Hi, doctor. Thank you so much for seeing me. Um, I hope I'm explaining this right, and please let me know if I'm not making sense, but... I guess I'm just having a little trouble lately with worrying too much?

Um, yes... I guess I do. I just try to be really responsible, you know? Being safe is a good thing, right? 

But, um... I hope you don't think I'm a bad employee or anything, but before I can actually take a ride, I sort of... I end up walking around the car a lot. Just checking the tires, the lights, the doors, making sure the meter and my paperwork are all okay.

Um, yeah... exactly. I just really want to be a safe driver, you know? I hope it doesn't make me sound paranoid or anything, but... yeah, I really do feel like doing all that is the only way to stop something terrible from happening.

It tells me that I missed something on the cab, and... and that somebody could get hurt because of me, or that my boss will think I lied about doing the inspection. \n\nIt's probably really silly, I know, but I just can't seem to make the thought shut up while I'm driving, or even when I'm just trying to study. Is... is that the kind of thing you can help with?\n\nDoctor: Do these thoughts about missing something on the cab feel like they are forcing their way into your mind, or are they more like worries that you're deliberately thinking about?\n\nPatient: Um... yeah, they definitely force their way in. I really don't want to be thinking about them at all. I hope I'm explaining this right, but... like I was just saying, even when I know for a fact I did my safety check, the thought just sort of hijacks my brain. \n\nIt just keeps hitting me out of nowhere that I missed something on the cab, and that someone's gonna get hurt, or my boss will say I lied. I try really, really hard to just focus on driving, or on my homework when I'm studying, but I just can't make it shut up. I'm definitely not doing it on purpose, I promise. Does... does that make sense? I'm sorry if I sound a little crazy.\n\nDoctor: When these thoughts about missing something on the cab come into your mind, do you feel like you need to do something to make them go away?\n\nPatient: Um, yes... I guess I do. I just try to be really responsible, you know? Being safe is a good thing, right? \n\nBut, um... I hope you don't think I'm a bad employee or anything, but before I can actually take a ride, I sort of... I end up walking around the car a lot. Just checking the tires, the lights, the doors, making sure the meter and my paperwork are all okay. \n\nThe thing is, I end up doing it over and over. And if it doesn't feel exactly right, I feel like I have to restart the whole entire check from the very beginning. I've even started taking pictures of everything with my phone just to prove to myself it's fine. It's... it's really embarrassing, honestly, because sometimes passengers are standing right there waiting for me, and I'm making myself late because I just can't stop checking. \n\nI know I shouldn't do it, and I'm really trying to be better about it, I promise. Am I... is that the kind of thing you were asking about? I hope I'm answering your question okay.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like walking around the car and re-checking everything is helping to prevent something bad from happening?\n\nPatient: Um, yeah... exactly. I just really want to be a safe driver, you know? I hope it doesn't make me sound paranoid or anything, but... yeah, I really do feel like doing all that is the only way to stop something terrible from happening. \n\nLike I was saying just a second ago about going around the car and checking the tires, the lights, the doors, the meter, and all my paperwork over and over... if I don't do it, or if I don't restart the whole check when it feels wrong, I just get this overwhelming feeling that I'm putting people in danger. Taking the pictures just... it makes me feel like I can prove I did my job right and kept everyone safe. \n\nEven though I know it makes me late and the passengers are just standing there waiting on me... which I know is terrible customer service, and I feel so, so guilty about it. I just... I feel like I'm protecting them, in a weird way? By making absolutely sure everything is perfect before we leave. Does that make sense? I really hope you don't think I'm completely crazy for doing all that.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your need to repeatedly check the car is causing problems in your daily life or relationships?\n\nPatient: Um... well, I mean... I really try not to let it get in the way. I want to be a good worker, I really do. But... yeah, I guess it is causing some issues. I hope you don't think I'm lazy or irresponsible, but... because I take so long checking everything, I've actually lost a few rides. My boss even gave me a warning about not getting on the road on time. \n\nAnd, um... I'm trying to study for my CDL, which is really important to me, but that keeps getting pushed back because... well, so much of my day just gets eaten up by all the checking. And honestly, I barely see my friends anymore. I'm always either behind on work or trying to catch up because of the delays. \n\nI know it sounds really bad, and I'm trying so hard to fix it, I promise. I want to do better. Is... is that what you were asking? I hope I'm not sounding like a complete failure.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel less anxious after you've finished checking the car?\n\nPatient: Um... yes, I do. For a little bit, at least. I mean, once I finally get through the whole routine and it feels right, and I have all my pictures on my phone... there's this wave of relief. Like, okay, I did it right, everyone is safe, I can finally just drive. \n\nBut, um... honestly, it doesn't last very long. As soon as I hit a bump in the road or, you know, someone gets in or out, I start wondering if I missed something. Or if something broke since I last checked. And then the worry just starts creeping back in. \n\nI really try to just focus on driving and being professional, I swear I do. I don't want to let the worry take over. But... yeah, the relief is pretty temporary. I hope that makes sense? I'm trying to explain it the best I can... is that a normal way to feel?\n\nDoctor: Is the feeling that you're protecting people by checking the car over and over again because you're afraid something specific will happen if you don't?\n\nPatient: Um... yeah, I guess there is. I really try to focus on the positive, I promise, but... well, that's actually kind of why I finally came in to see someone. Because even when I know I did my safety check—like, I know for a fact I did it right—this thought just keeps hitting me. It just pops into my head that I missed something on the cab. \n\nAnd... I'm so scared that because I missed something, somebody could get hurt. Or... or even that my boss will find out and say I lied about doing the check in the first place. I would never lie to my boss, I really try to be a trustworthy employee, but... I just can't make the thought shut up. It just stays there in my head while I'm driving, or when I'm at home trying to study. \n\nI try so hard to push it away and just focus on my job. I hope you don't think I'm a bad person or a dangerous driver for having thoughts like that? I really am trying my absolute best to just do things right. Is it... is it okay that I'm telling you all this?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. My boss even gave me a warning about not getting on the road on time. \n\nAnd, um... People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um... emotional pain? I... I'm not really sure if I'd use those exact words. I mean, I definitely don't want to sound dramatic or like I'm just sitting around whining about it. I know a lot of people have things way worse than I do, and I really do try to stay positive! 

It's... well, it's definitely stressful, sure. Especially when I let my boss down or keep my passengers waiting, like I was saying before. That makes me feel really guilty. But I wouldn't say I'm in, like, deep emotional pain or agony or anything like that. I just feel bad that I'm not better at handling it all yet. 

Does... does that make sense? I hope that's an okay answer. I just really don't want you to think I'm feeling sorry for myself or not trying hard enough to fix it. I promise I'm trying to push through it as best as I can! Is that kind of what you were asking about?

I hope I'm not being too confusing.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your worrying and checking behaviors are causing you a lot of distress or emotional pain?\n\nPatient: Um... emotional pain? I... I'm not really sure if I'd use those exact words. I mean, I definitely don't want to sound dramatic or like I'm just sitting around whining about it. I know a lot of people have things way worse than I do, and I really do try to stay positive! \n\nIt's... well, it's definitely stressful, sure. Especially when I let my boss down or keep my passengers waiting, like I was saying before. That makes me feel really guilty. But I wouldn't say I'm in, like, deep emotional pain or agony or anything like that. I just feel bad that I'm not better at handling it all yet. \n\nDoes... does that make sense? I hope that's an okay answer. I just really don't want you to think I'm feeling sorry for myself or not trying hard enough to fix it. I promise I'm trying to push through it as best as I can! Is that kind of what you were asking about?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your worrying and checking behaviors are significantly interfering with your ability to function at work or enjoy your free time?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... wow, I'm really sorry. I really don't mean to waste your time or be difficult, I promise! I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right information. \n\nBut, um, yes, it does interfere. Like I mentioned a minute ago, I've... I've lost rides and gotten warnings from my boss because I just can't get on the road on time with everything I have to do. And my studying for my CDL keeps getting pushed back because so much of my day just gets eaten up by it all. And for my free time... well, I barely see my friends anymore. I'm usually just too behind on work or trying to catch up after all the checking delays. \n\nI know that probably sounds really bad, but I swear I'm trying my hardest to get it under control! People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um... oh, wow, you're asking about the order... I, um... I hope I don't sound too weird or like I'm completely losing it when I explain this. I really just want to make sure the car is safe for people, you know? I'd hate to be responsible for someone getting hurt. 

But, um, yes... there is a certain way I have to do it. Before I can take a ride, I have to go around the car and check everything. The tires, the lights, the doors, the meter, the paperwork... I just keep checking them over and over. And I have to do it in a specific way, or it just... it doesn't feel right. Sometimes I even take pictures on my phone just to prove to myself that I checked it. 

And, um... if something feels off, or if I lose track of the order, I have to start the whole thing completely over. Even... even if passengers are already waiting and I'm running late. I know that sounds awful, and I really, really don't want to inconvenience my passengers! I feel so bad when they have to wait. I just... I can't seem to stop until it feels right. 

Does that make sense? I hope I'm not sounding too crazy. I really am trying to be a good driver, I promise. Is that the kind of thing you were asking about?

I'm trying to explain it the best I can... is that a normal way to feel?\n\nDoctor: Is the feeling that you're protecting people by checking the car over and over again because you're afraid something specific will happen if you don't?\n\nPatient: Um... yeah, I guess there is. I really try to focus on the positive, I promise, but... well, that's actually kind of why I finally came in to see someone. I hope it doesn't make me sound paranoid or anything, but... yeah, I really do feel like doing all that is the only way to stop something terrible from happening. \n\nLike I was saying just a second ago about going around the car and checking the tires, the lights, the doors, the meter, and all my paperwork over and over... if I don't do it, or if I don't restart the whole check when it feels wrong, I just get this overwhelming feeling that I'm putting people in danger. Taking the pictures just... it makes me feel like I can prove I did my job right and kept everyone safe. \n\nEven though I know it makes me late and the passengers are just standing there waiting on me... which I know is terrible customer service, and I feel so, so guilty about it. I just... I feel like I'm protecting them, in a weird way? By making absolutely sure everything is perfect before we leave. Does that make sense? I really hope you don't think I'm completely crazy for doing all that.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your need to repeatedly check the car is causing problems in your daily life or relationships?\n\nPatient: Um... well, I mean... I really try not to let it get in the way. I want to be a good worker, I really do. But... yeah, I guess it is causing some issues. I hope you don't think I'm lazy or irresponsible, but... because I take so long checking everything, I've actually lost a few rides. My boss even gave me a warning about not getting on the road on time. \n\nAnd, um... I'm trying to study for my CDL, which is really important to me, but that keeps getting pushed back because... well, so much of my day just gets eaten up by all the checking. And honestly, I barely see my friends anymore. I'm always either behind on work or trying to catch up because of the delays. \n\nI know it sounds really bad, and I'm trying so hard to fix it, I promise. I want to do better. Is... is that what you were asking? I hope I'm not sounding like a complete failure.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel less anxious after you've finished checking the car?\n\nPatient: Um... yes, I do. For a little bit, at least. I mean, once I finally get through the whole routine and it feels right, and I have all my pictures on my phone... there's this wave of relief. Like, okay, I did it right, everyone is safe, I can finally just drive. \n\nBut, um... honestly, it doesn't last very long. As soon as I hit a bump in the road or, you know, someone gets in or out, I start wondering if I missed something. Or if something broke since I last checked. And then the worry just starts creeping back in. \n\nI really try to just focus on driving and being professional, I swear I do. I don't want to let the worry take over. But... yeah, the relief is pretty temporary. I hope that makes sense? I'm trying to explain it the best I can... is that a normal way to feel?\n\nDoctor: Is the feeling that you're protecting people by checking the car over and over again because you're afraid something specific will happen if you don't?\n\nPatient: Um... yeah, I guess there is. I really try to focus on the positive, I promise, but... well, that's actually kind of why I finally came in to see someone. Because even when I know I did my safety check—like, I know for a fact I did it right—this thought just keeps hitting me. It just pops into my head that I missed something on the cab. \n\nAnd... I'm so scared that because I missed something, somebody could get hurt. Or... or even that my boss will find out and say I lied about doing the check in the first place. I would never lie to my boss, I really try to be a trustworthy employee, but... I just can't make the thought shut up. It just stays there in my head while I'm driving, or when I'm at home trying to study. \n\nI try so hard to push it away and just focus on my job. I hope you don't think I'm a bad person or a dangerous driver for having thoughts like that? I really am trying my absolute best to just do things right. Is it... is it okay that I'm telling you all this?\n\nDoctor: Is the feeling that you need to check the car over and over again because you're afraid of something specific happening, like an accident, or is it more about feeling responsible for everything being perfect?\n\nPatient: Um... well, it's... I guess it's a little bit of both? But maybe more about just making sure nobody gets hurt. I mean, I don't need things to be, like, perfectly clean or anything like that. I just... I want to be a safe driver. I want to do a good job for my boss and my passengers. \n\nI just get so worried about, you know, an accident happening because I missed a step. I hope I'm answering your question the right way.\n\nDoctor: Is your need to check the car over and over again more about fearing that you'll make a mistake or fearing what might happen if you do make a mistake?\n\nPatient: Um... I really try to just push it away, I promise I do. I hope that answers what you were asking? I want to make sure I'm giving you the right information.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're checking the car over and over because you need to be in control of the situation or because you're afraid of something unexpected happening?\n\nPatient: Um... I'm sorry if that sounds really weird or unprofessional.\n\nDoctor: Is your need to check the car repeatedly more about the possibility of making a mistake itself or the potential consequences of that mistake?\n\nPatient: Um... Um... well, it's definitely not about trying to control everything. I really hope it doesn't seem like I'm some sort of control freak! I just... I want to be a good driver. It's really just about being afraid of what could happen if I miss something. 

Like I was saying before—and I'm really sorry to keep bringing it up, I hope I'm not boring you—but that's why I keep going around the car, checking the tires, the lights, the doors, the meter, the paperwork... just over and over. And taking the pictures on my phone. It's just because I'm so worried something will go wrong and someone will get hurt because of me. 

That's why I have to restart the whole check if it doesn't feel right, even when I know the passengers are waiting and I'm already running late. I feel terrible about making them wait, I really do. I just... I can't shake that fear of something bad happening if I don't check it perfectly. Does that make sense? I hope I'm answering your question okay.
